<tr><td colspan="7">例 His oafish manners at dinner offended the host.  他晚餐时粗笨的举止得罪了主人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opaque</td><td>/oʊˈpeɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透明的；晦涩难懂的</td><td>opaque legal language<br>晦涩的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regulations remain opaque even to experienced tax lawyers.  这些法规连资深税务律师都觉得晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 senseless</td><td>/ˈsensləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，愚蠢的</td><td>senseless violence<br>毫无意义的暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the safety budget now would be completely senseless.  现在削减安全预算完全是愚蠢之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unintelligent</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnˈtɛlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不聪明的，愚笨的</td><td>an unintelligent remark<br>愚笨的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The character is lazy but by no means unintelligent.  这个角色懒惰，但绝不愚笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vacuous</td><td>/ˈvækjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茫然的，愚蠢的</td><td>a vacuous stare<br>茫然的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the difficult question with a vacuous stare and a shrug.  面对这个难题，他只是茫然地望着，耸了耸肩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insightful</td><td>/ˈɪnˌsaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有洞察力的，有见识的</td><td>an insightful comment<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her insightful reading of the poem impressed the whole class.  她对这首诗富有洞见的解读让全班佩服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apt</td><td>/æpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，合适的；聪明的</td><td>an apt description<br>恰当的描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an apt description of how the meeting actually went.  那是对会议实际情况的一个恰当描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brainy</td><td>/ˈbreɪni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，头脑好的</td><td>a brainy student<br>聪明的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brainy student solved the puzzle in two minutes.  那个聪明的学生两分钟就解开了谜题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fast</td><td>/fæst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快的</td><td>a fast runner<br>跑得快的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a fast worker who finishes every job ahead of schedule.  他做事很快，每项工作都提前完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intelligent</td><td>/ɪnˈtelədʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，聪明的</td><td>an intelligent decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old plant was an intelligent decision.  卖掉旧厂是个明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keen</td><td>/ˈkiːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏锐的，锐利的</td><td>a keen eye for detail<br>对细节的敏锐眼光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographer has a keen eye for unusual light.  这位摄影师对特别的光线有敏锐的眼力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nimble</td><td>/ˈnɪmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏捷的，灵活的</td><td>nimble fingers<br>灵活的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her nimble fingers tied the knot in one second.  她灵活的手指一秒钟就打好了结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quick</td><td>/ˈkwɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快的，迅速的</td><td>a quick decision<br>迅速的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A quick decision saved the patient's life that night.  一个迅速的决定在那晚救了病人的命。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quick-witted</td><td>/ˌkwɪk ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机智的，反应快的</td><td>a quick-witted reply<br>机智的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quick-witted intern saved the presentation with one remark.  机智的实习生用一句话救了那场演示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharp-witted</td><td>/ˈʃɑrp ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，头脑敏锐的</td><td>a sharp-witted reply<br>机敏的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sharp-witted reply silenced the whole panel.  她机敏的回答让整个评审团哑然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>obviate</b></td><td>/ˈɑːbvieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>排除，使不必要</td><td>obviate the need for<br>使……不再必要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short online form would obviate the need for three separate interviews.  一份简短的在线表格就能使三次单独面谈不再必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avert</td><td>/əˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，防止；转移（目光）</td><td>avert a disaster<br>避免灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quick action by the crew averted a serious accident.  船员的迅速行动避免了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forestall</td><td>/fɔːrˈstɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先阻止，抢先防止</td><td>forestall criticism<br>预先化解批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered early to forestall further criticism.  她提前作答以预先阻止更多批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preclude</td><td>/prɪˈkluːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先阻止</td><td>preclude further discussion<br>阻止进一步讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A lack of funding does not preclude us from starting the project.  资金不足并不能阻止我们启动这个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stave off</td><td>/ˈsteɪv ˈɔf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>避开，暂时挡住</td><td>stave off hunger<br>暂时挡住饥饿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A handful of nuts staved off hunger until dinner.  一把坚果挡住了到晚餐前的饥饿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 head off</td><td>/ˈhɛd ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>阻止，防止发生</td><td>head off a crisis<br>避免一场危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early talks headed off a crisis at the plant.  及早的谈判避免了厂里的一场危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>occult</b></td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opaque</td><td>/oʊˈpeɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透明的；晦涩难懂的</td><td>opaque legal language<br>晦涩的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regulations remain opaque even to experienced tax lawyers.  这些法规连资深税务律师都觉得晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impenetrable</td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>impenetrable jargon<br>晦涩难懂的行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was written in impenetrable jargon that confused everyone.  合同用晦涩难懂的行话写成，让所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bare</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>揭露，袒露；赤裸的</td><td>bare the truth<br>揭露真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the interview he bared his family's darkest secrets.  在采访中他揭露了家族最黑暗的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 readily fathomable</del></td><td>/ˈrɛdɪli ˈfæðəməbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易理解的</td><td>readily fathomable rules<br>容易理解的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manual states readily fathomable rules for new users.  手册为新用户给出容易理解的规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>odium</b></td><td>/ˈoʊdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>憎恶，讨厌</td><td>public odium<br>公众的憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public odium followed the executives long after the company had been dissolved.  公司解散很久之后，公众的憎恶仍伴随着这些高管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignominy</td><td>/ˈɪɡnəmɪni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>耻辱</td><td>the ignominy of defeat<br>失败的耻辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He could not bear the ignominy of being dismissed so publicly.  他无法承受如此公开地被解职的耻辱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 obloquy</del></td><td>/ˈɑːbləkwi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谩骂，诽谤</td><td>suffer public obloquy<br>遭受公众谩骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The former minister suffered public obloquy for years after the scandal broke.  丑闻爆发后，这位前部长多年遭受公众谩骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opprobrium</td><td>/əˈproʊbriəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>公开谴责，耻辱</td><td>public opprobrium<br>公众的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor faced weeks of opprobrium after the decision.  市长在那个决定之后面对数周的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hankering</td><td>/ˈhæŋkərɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>渴望，向往</td><td>a hankering for home<br>对家的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt a sudden hankering for his grandmother's cooking.  他忽然渴望祖母做的饭菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infatuation</td><td>/ɪnˌfætʃuˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迷恋，热恋</td><td>a brief infatuation<br>短暂的迷恋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her infatuation with the city faded after one winter.  她对这座城市的迷恋在一个冬天后消退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>offbeat</b></td><td>/ˌɔːfˈbiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平常的</td><td>an offbeat sense of humor<br>别致的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offbeat sense of humor won over an audience that had arrived skeptical.  他别致的幽默感赢得了原本抱着怀疑到场的观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 queer</td><td>/ˈkwɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇特的</td><td>a queer feeling<br>奇怪的感觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A queer feeling told her the house was empty.  一种奇怪的感觉告诉她房子是空的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaccustomed</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈkʌstəmd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不习惯的；不常见的</td><td>unaccustomed to the cold<br>不习惯寒冷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unaccustomed to the cold, he wore three sweaters indoors.  他不习惯寒冷，在室内穿了三件毛衣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncommon</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不常见的，罕有的</td><td>not uncommon<br>并不罕见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such mistakes are not uncommon among beginners.  这类错误在初学者中并不罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uncustomary</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkʌstəmeri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合惯例的，罕有的</td><td>uncustomary frankness<br>少见的坦率</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke with uncustomary frankness about his failures.  他一反常态地坦率谈起自己的失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 out-of-the-way</td><td>/ˌaʊt əv ðə ˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，稀奇的；偏僻的</td><td>out-of-the-way facts<br>稀奇少见的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She collects out-of-the-way facts that surprise even seasoned quiz players.  她收集稀奇少见的知识，连资深答题者都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 bathetic</del></td><td>/bəˈθetɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故作伤感的；突降的</td><td>a bathetic ending<br>故作伤感的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel ends with a bathetic scene of sudden tears.  小说以一个突然落泪、故作伤感的场面收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 usual</td><td>/ˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>通常的，惯常的</td><td>the usual routine<br>惯常的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at his usual time and took his usual seat.  他按老时间到达，坐在老位子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>offhand</b></td><td>/ˌɔːfˈhænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>即兴的，没有准备的</td><td>an offhand remark<br>随口的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An offhand remark about the budget caused three days of angry headlines.  一句关于预算的随口评论引来三天的愤怒头条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ad-lib</td><td>/ˌædˈlɪb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>即兴的；即兴表演</td><td>an ad-lib remark<br>即兴的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The host had to ad-lib for ten minutes when the guest was late.  嘉宾迟到时主持人只好即兴撑了十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 extemporary</del></td><td>/ɪkˈstɛmpəˌrɛri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>即席的，无准备的</td><td>an extemporary speech<br>即席演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an extemporary talk that impressed the whole audience.  她做了一场即兴演讲，令全场听众印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impromptu</td><td>/ɪmˈprɑːmptuː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>即席的表演；即席的，即兴的</td><td>an impromptu performance<br>即兴表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band gave an impromptu concert in the town square.  乐队在镇广场上举行了一场即兴音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 improvised</td><td>/ˈɪmprəˌvaɪzd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>即兴的，临时凑成的</td><td>an improvised speech<br>即兴的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They slept in an improvised shelter made of branches.  他们睡在用树枝临时搭成的棚子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unplanned</td><td>/ənˈplænd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无计划的，意外的</td><td>an unplanned detour<br>计划外的绕行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unplanned stop in the village became the trip's highlight.  在村里的一次临时停留成了旅行的亮点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unpremeditated</td><td>/ˌʌnpriˈmedɪteɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非预谋的，未事先计划的</td><td>an unpremeditated attack<br>非预谋的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court decided the killing was sudden and unpremeditated.  法庭认定这起杀人是突发且非预谋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unrehearsed</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈhɜːrst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未经排练的，即兴的</td><td>an unrehearsed performance<br>未经排练的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unrehearsed answer was more convincing than any script.  她未经准备的回答比任何稿子都更有说服力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considered</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思的，慎重的</td><td>a considered opinion<br>深思后的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is my considered judgment after reading the whole report.  这是我读完整份报告后的慎重判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 planned</td><td>/ˈplænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>计划好的，有计划的</td><td>a carefully planned trip<br>精心策划的旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole ceremony was planned down to the last detail.  整个典礼被计划到了最后一个细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 premeditated</td><td>/priˈmedəˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>预谋的，事先策划的</td><td>premeditated murder<br>预谋杀人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Prosecutors argued that the robbery was carefully premeditated.  检察官认为这起抢劫是精心预谋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prepared</td><td>/priˈperd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>准备好的</td><td>be well prepared<br>做好充分准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Come prepared with questions for tomorrow's interview.  为明天的面试准备好问题再来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rehearsed</td><td>/rɪˈhɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>排练过的，事先准备好的</td><td>a carefully rehearsed answer<br>精心准备的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her apology sounded rehearsed rather than sincere.  她的道歉听起来像是排练过的，而不真诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>officious</b></td><td>/əˈfɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多管闲事的</td><td>an officious clerk<br>爱管闲事的职员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An officious clerk insisted we fill out two forms we clearly did not need.  一位爱管闲事的职员非要我们填两份明显不需要的表格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interfering</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈfɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>干涉的，爱插手的</td><td>an interfering relative<br>爱插手的亲戚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An interfering aunt rearranged their whole kitchen.  一位爱插手的姑妈把他们厨房全重摆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intruding</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtruːdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>侵入的，打扰的</td><td>an intruding noise<br>扰人的噪音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An intruding noise from the street spoiled the recording.  街上扰人的噪音毁了这次录音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meddlesome</td><td>/ˈmedəlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱干涉的，多事的</td><td>a meddlesome neighbor<br>多事的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their meddlesome neighbor reported every small noise.  他们多事的邻居举报了每一点小声响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obtrusive</td><td>/əbˈtruːsɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扎眼的，难看显眼的</td><td>an obtrusive advertisement<br>扎眼的广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An obtrusive advertisement covered half the historic facade of the old theater.  一幅扎眼的广告遮住了老剧院半面历史立面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presuming</td><td>/prɪˈzuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自以为是的，冒失放肆的</td><td>a presuming stranger<br>冒失的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was presuming of him to offer advice uninvited.  他未经邀请就给建议，实在冒失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prying</td><td>/ˈpraɪɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱打探的，窥探的</td><td>prying neighbors<br>爱打探的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drew the curtains to escape prying eyes.  她拉上窗帘以避开窥探的目光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snoopy</td><td>/ˈsnuːpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱窥探的，好打探的</td><td>a snoopy landlady<br>爱窥探的女房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their snoopy landlady read the postcards before delivering them.  他们爱窥探的女房东送信前先读明信片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unobtrusive</td><td>/ˌʌnəbˈtruːsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不显眼的，不张扬的</td><td>unobtrusive service<br>不张扬的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel's unobtrusive service won it loyal guests.  这家酒店不张扬的服务赢得了忠实客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>offish</b></td><td>/ˈɔːfɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，不友好的</td><td>seem a bit offish<br>显得有点冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new neighbors seemed offish until we invited them over.  在我们邀请之前，新邻居显得有些冷淡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aloof</td><td>/əˈluːf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，冷漠孤傲的</td><td>remain aloof from<br>对…保持冷淡疏远</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed aloof from office gossip and rarely joined lunch.  他对办公室的闲话保持冷淡，很少参加午餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detached</td><td>/dɪˈtætʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠超然的，不介入的</td><td>a detached attitude<br>超然冷漠的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the accident in a strangely detached voice.  她用一种异常冷漠的语气描述了那场事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distant</td><td>/ˈdɪstənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，疏远的；遥远的</td><td>a distant manner<br>冷淡的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was polite but distant throughout the whole dinner.  整个晚餐期间她客气却冷淡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remote</td><td>/rɪˈmoʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，疏离的；遥远的</td><td>a remote expression<br>疏离的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His manner remained remote even among close family members.  即使在近亲之间他也显得疏离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unbending</td><td>/ʌnˈbendɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不亲切的；不妥协的</td><td>an unbending attitude<br>不肯通融的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old teacher was unbending about rules and manners.  这位老教师在规矩和礼节上毫不通融。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsociable</td><td>/ʌnˈsoʊʃəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不爱交际的</td><td>an unsociable person<br>不爱交际的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long years of study had left him rather unsociable.  多年苦读使他变得相当不爱交际。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sociable</td><td>/ˈsoʊʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好交际的，友善的</td><td>a sociable personality<br>好交际的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is far more sociable than her quiet older brother.  她比她安静的哥哥爱交际得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cordial</td><td>/ˈkɔːrdʒəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情友好的；令人振奋的</td><td>a cordial welcome<br>热情的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two leaders exchanged cordial greetings before the talks.  两位领导人在会谈前互致亲切的问候。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 friendly</td><td>/ˈfrendli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>友好的，亲切的</td><td>a friendly welcome<br>友好的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shopkeeper was friendly and remembered every regular customer.  店主很友好，记得每位常客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 social</td><td>/ˈsoʊʃəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱交际的；社会的</td><td>a social animal<br>群居动物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Humans are social creatures who need regular company and talk.  人是需要经常陪伴与交谈的社会性生物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warm</td><td>/ˈwɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，亲切的</td><td>a warm welcome<br>热情的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers gave the travelers a warm welcome.  村民热情欢迎了这些旅人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>offkey</b></del></td><td>/ˌɔːfˈkiː/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，不合适的</td><td>an offkey remark<br>不合适的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offkey remark about her divorce silenced the entire dinner table.  他关于她离婚的不合适言论让整桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ominous</b></td><td>/ˈɑːmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶兆的</td><td>an ominous silence<br>不祥的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ominous silence fell over the crowd as the announcement began.  宣布开始时，人群陷入不祥的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baleful</td><td>/ˈbeɪlfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>a baleful influence<br>有害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His baleful influence on the young players worried the coach.  他对年轻球员的有害影响让教练担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foreboding</td><td>/fɔːrˈboʊdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不祥的；不祥的预感</td><td>a sense of foreboding<br>不祥的预感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt a strange foreboding as the sky darkened.  天色转暗时她有种奇怪的不祥预感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inauspicious</td><td>/ˌɪnɔːˈspɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不吉利的，不祥的</td><td>an inauspicious start<br>不吉利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team made an inauspicious start by losing twice at home.  球队主场连输两场，开局不利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portentous</td><td>/pɔːrˈtentəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>预兆性的，凶兆的</td><td>a portentous silence<br>预示不祥的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A portentous silence fell over the room before the verdict was read.  宣读判决之前，房间里陷入一阵预示不祥的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sinister</td><td>/ˈsɪnɪstər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴险的，不祥的</td><td>a sinister smile<br>阴险的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was something sinister about the empty old house.  那座空荡的老房子有种阴森之感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threatening</td><td>/ˈθretənɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威胁的；（天气）要变坏的</td><td>a threatening gesture<br>威胁性的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a threatening gesture and then walked quickly away.  他做了个威胁的手势，然后快步走开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 minatory</del></td><td>/ˈmɪnətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>带来威胁的，有凶兆的</td><td>a minatory tone<br>带威胁的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sent a minatory letter demanding payment within one week.  他寄来一封带威胁的信，要求一周内付款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 auspicious</td><td>/ɔːˈspɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好兆头的</td><td>an auspicious beginning<br>吉利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain on the wedding day was considered an auspicious beginning.  婚礼当天下雨被认为是个好兆头的开端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unthreatening</td><td>/ˌʌnˈθrɛtənɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不具威胁的，温和的</td><td>an unthreatening manner<br>毫无威胁感的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unthreatening manner made the children relax at once.  她毫无威胁感的态度让孩子们立刻放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>omniscient</b></td><td>/ɑːmˈnɪʃənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无所不知的</td><td>an omniscient narrator<br>全知的叙述者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel uses an omniscient narrator who knows every character's private thoughts.  这部小说用了一位知晓每个角色私密想法的全知叙述者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vacuous</td><td>/ˈvækjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茫然的，愚蠢的</td><td>a vacuous stare<br>茫然的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the difficult question with a vacuous stare and a shrug.  面对这个难题，他只是茫然地望着，耸了耸肩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>onerous</b></td><td>/ˈɑːnərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>onerous responsibilities<br>繁重的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for the archive proved more onerous than the volunteers had expected.  照管这份档案比志愿者们预想的更费时费心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arduous</td><td>/ˈɑːrdʒuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以做到的，费劲的</td><td>an arduous journey<br>艰苦的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climb proved far more arduous than any of them expected.  这次攀登远比他们任何人预想的都费劲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burdensome</td><td>/ˈbɜːrdənsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，累人的</td><td>burdensome regulations<br>繁琐的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new paperwork proved burdensome for small rural clinics.  新的文书工作对乡村小诊所来说很繁重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 challenging</td><td>/ˈtʃæləndʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有挑战性的，费心力的</td><td>a challenging assignment<br>有挑战性的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor gave us a challenging assignment due next week.  教授给了我们一个下周交的有挑战性作业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，苛求的；费时间花心思的</td><td>exacting quality standards<br>严格的质量标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The airline maintains exacting standards for every part it installs.  这家航空公司对安装的每个部件都保持严格的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grueling</td><td>/ˈɡruːəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>累人的，折磨人的</td><td>a grueling schedule<br>折磨人的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team endured a grueling schedule of twelve games in three weeks.  球队熬过了三周十二场比赛这种折磨人的赛程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taxing</td><td>/ˈtæksɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>a taxing task<br>繁重的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for two infants proved a taxing task for the young couple.  照顾两个婴儿对这对年轻夫妻是繁重的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 toilsome</del></td><td>/ˈtɔɪlsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辛苦的，费力的</td><td>toilsome labor<br>辛苦的劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying manuscripts by hand was toilsome work that ruined many eyes.  手抄书稿是毁坏许多人眼睛的辛苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 requiring little effort</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪrɪŋ ˈlɪtəl ˈɛfərt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不费力的</td><td>a task requiring little effort<br>毫不费力的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sorting the cards is a task requiring little effort.  给卡片分类是毫不费力的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nondemanding</del></td><td>/ˌnɑndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不苛求的，轻松的</td><td>a nondemanding job<br>轻松的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose a nondemanding job to leave time for painting.  她选了份轻松的工作，好留出时间画画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unchallenging</td><td>/ˌʌnˈtʃælɪndʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有挑战性的，轻松的</td><td>an unchallenging job<br>没有挑战的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He quit the unchallenging position after only three months.  他只干了三个月就辞掉那份没有挑战性的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undemanding</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>要求不高的，轻松的</td><td>an undemanding course<br>要求不高的课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose an undemanding role so she could rest more.  她选了个轻松的角色以便多休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>opaque</b></td><td>/oʊˈpeɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不透明的；晦涩难懂的</td><td>opaque legal language<br>晦涩的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regulations remain opaque even to experienced tax lawyers.  这些法规连资深税务律师都觉得晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accessible</td><td>/əkˈsesəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可以到达的；可理解的</td><td>an accessible explanation<br>易懂的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He makes physics accessible to ordinary readers.  他让普通读者也能理解物理学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unambiguous</td><td>/ˌʌnæmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，毫不含糊的</td><td>unambiguous written instructions<br>明确的书面指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract gives unambiguous rules about late payment.  合同就迟付款给出了明确规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unequivocal</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不含糊的，明确的</td><td>an unequivocal denial<br>毫不含糊的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board issued an unequivocal statement supporting the director.  董事会发表了明确支持主任的声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>opine</b></td><td>/oʊˈpaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>发表意见；认为</td><td>opine on the matter<br>就此事发表看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several economists opined on the matter without agreeing on a single figure.  几位经济学家就此事发表看法，却未就任何一个数字达成一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comment</td><td>/ˈkɑːment/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>评论，发表意见</td><td>comment on the report<br>评论那份报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials declined to comment on the ongoing investigation.  官员拒绝就正在进行的调查发表评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 editorialize</td><td>/ˌedəˈtɔːriəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>发表社论；加入个人观点</td><td>editorialize about politics<br>就政治大发议论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reporter began to editorialize instead of stating facts.  这名记者开始发表个人议论，而非陈述事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 note</td><td>/ˈnoʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>指出，提到；注意</td><td>note that the data differ<br>指出数据有差异</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge noted that both accounts were plainly inconsistent.  法官指出两份陈述明显互相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 observe</td><td>/əbˈzɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守；观察</td><td>observe the law<br>遵守法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All visitors are expected to observe the museum's rules.  所有参观者都应遵守博物馆的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reflect</td><td>/rɪˈflekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>反映，显露；思考</td><td>reflect on the outcome<br>反思结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rising sales figures reflect a real change in consumer confidence.  销售数字上升反映出消费者信心的真实变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weigh in</td><td>/weɪ ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发表意见，参与讨论</td><td>weigh in on the debate<br>参与辩论发言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several economists weighed in on the proposed tax reform.  几位经济学家就拟议的税改发表了意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>opportune</b></td><td>/ˌɑːpərˈtuːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的，适当的</td><td>an opportune moment<br>恰当的时机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She waited for an opportune moment to raise the question of salary.  她等着一个恰当的时机提出薪资问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seasonable</td><td>/ˈsiːzənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合时的，及时的</td><td>seasonable advice<br>及时的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His seasonable warning saved the crop from frost damage.  他及时的警告使庄稼免遭霜害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-timed</td><td>/ˌwɛl ˈtaɪmd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>时机恰当的</td><td>a well-timed intervention<br>时机恰当的干预</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her well-timed joke eased the tension in the room.  她时机恰当的玩笑缓解了室内的紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconvenient</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈviːniənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不方便的，造成麻烦的</td><td>at an inconvenient time<br>在不方便的时候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She called at an inconvenient time, just before dinner.  她在不方便的时候打来电话，正好在晚饭前。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untimely</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪmli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合时宜的；过早的</td><td>an untimely interruption<br>不合时宜的打断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The untimely rain ruined the outdoor ceremony completely.  不合时宜的大雨彻底毁了露天典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inappropriate</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈproʊpriət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适当的，不合适的</td><td>inappropriate behavior at work<br>工作中的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing shorts to the interview was clearly inappropriate.  穿短裤去面试显然不合适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unseasonable</td><td>/ʌnˈsizənəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合季节的；不合时宜的</td><td>unseasonable warmth in January<br>一月里反常的暖和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unseasonable heat in March confused the valley's fruit trees.  三月反常的高温让谷中的果树错乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>opprobrious</b></del></td><td>/əˈproʊbriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辱骂的；臭名昭著的，可耻的</td><td>opprobrious remarks<br>辱骂性的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet was filled with opprobrious attacks on the mayor.  那本小册子充满了对市长的辱骂性攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scurrilous</td><td>/ˈskɜːrələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶语中伤的，下流的</td><td>scurrilous accusations<br>下流的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet contained scurrilous attacks on every member of the council.  那本小册子对议会每位成员都有下流的攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitriolic</td><td>/ˌvɪtriˈɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）刻薄的</td><td>a vitriolic attack<br>尖刻的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review was so vitriolic that the author stopped writing.  那篇评论如此刻薄，作者因此停笔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vituperative</td><td>/ˌvaɪˈtuːpərətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谩骂的，斥责的</td><td>a vituperative attack<br>谩骂式的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vituperative letter to the editor was never printed.  他写给编辑的那封谩骂信从未刊出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contumelious</del></td><td>/ˌkɑntjuˈmiliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢无礼的，辱骂的</td><td>contumelious remarks<br>傲慢辱骂的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His contumelious tone offended nearly everyone at the meeting.  他傲慢无礼的语气几乎冒犯了会上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discreditable</td><td>/dɪsˈkrɛdɪtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>discreditable conduct<br>不光彩的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee found his discreditable conduct unworthy of a public official.  委员会认为他不光彩的行为不配做公职人员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgraceful</td><td>/dɪsˈɡreɪsfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耻的，不体面的</td><td>disgraceful behavior<br>可耻的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their disgraceful conduct cost the team its main sponsorship.  他们可耻的行为使球队失去了主要赞助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonorable</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>a dishonorable discharge<br>不光彩的除役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Betraying an old friend for money is plainly dishonorable.  为钱背叛老友显然可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignominious</td><td>/ˌɪɡnəˈmɪniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耻的，不光彩的</td><td>an ignominious defeat<br>可耻的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The season ended in ignominious defeat at home.  这个赛季以主场可耻的失利告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamous</td><td>/ˈɪnfəməs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭名昭著的</td><td>an infamous crime<br>臭名昭著的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dictator remains infamous for the brutal purges he ordered during his rule.  这位独裁者因其统治期间下令进行的残酷清洗而臭名昭著。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 notorious</td><td>/noʊˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>声名狼藉的</td><td>notorious for corruption<br>因腐败而臭名昭著</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighborhood was notorious for its late-night noise and crime.  这个街区因深夜噪音和犯罪而声名狼藉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shameful</td><td>/ˈʃeɪmfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耻的，丢脸的</td><td>a shameful defeat<br>可耻的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was shameful to abandon the injured climber on the ridge.  把受伤的登山者丢在山脊上很可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unrespectable</del></td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈspɛktəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不体面的，不受敬重的</td><td>unrespectable company<br>不体面的交往圈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drifted into unrespectable circles soon after leaving school.  离校后他很快混进了不体面的圈子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irreproachable</td><td>/ˌɪrɪˈproʊtʃəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无可指责的，无瑕的</td><td>irreproachable conduct<br>无可指责的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His record as a judge was irreproachable for thirty long years.  他担任法官三十年的记录无可指责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honorable</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光荣的，可敬的</td><td>an honorable retreat<br>光荣的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made the honorable choice and returned all the money.  她做出可敬的选择，把钱全退了回去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reputable</td><td>/ˈrepjətəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>声誉好的，可信的</td><td>a reputable dealer<br>信誉良好的经销商</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buy the parts from a reputable supplier rather than an auction site.  从信誉良好的供应商买零件，而不是拍卖网站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respectable</td><td>/rɪˈspektəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>体面的，值得尊敬的</td><td>a respectable middle-class family<br>体面的中产家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They came from a respectable family with an excellent local reputation.  他们出身于当地声誉极佳的体面家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 good repute</td><td>/ɡʊd rɪˈpjut/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好名声，好声誉</td><td>a man of good repute<br>声誉良好的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm has long enjoyed good repute among local builders.  这家公司在当地建筑商中素有好声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>outrageous</b></td><td>/aʊtˈreɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，难以容忍的；极坏的，极可怕的</td><td>an outrageous price<br>离谱的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charging fifty dollars for a sandwich seems outrageous to most travelers.  一个三明治收五十美元，对大多数旅客来说太离谱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baroque</td><td>/bəˈroʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>装饰华丽的，过分雕琢的，复杂的</td><td>baroque ornamentation<br>华丽繁复的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceiling was covered with heavy baroque ornamentation in gold.  天花板上覆满了华丽繁复的金色装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exorbitant</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːrbɪtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度的</td><td>exorbitant prices<br>过高的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists complained about exorbitant prices for bottled water near the temple.  游客抱怨寺庙附近瓶装水价格过高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extravagant</td><td>/ɪkˈstrævəɡənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的；过度的，不必要的</td><td>extravagant spending<br>挥霍的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overdue</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈduː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过期的，逾期的；早该发生的</td><td>an overdue payment<br>逾期的付款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library fined her for three books that were badly overdue.  图书馆因三本严重逾期的书对她罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconscionable</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːnʃənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，不合理的；不道德的</td><td>unconscionable delay<br>过分的拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charging that fee for a five-minute repair seems unconscionable to me.  五分钟的维修收那笔费用在我看来太过分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heinous</td><td>/ˈheɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，十恶不赦的</td><td>a heinous act<br>令人憎恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as heinous and utterly without excuse.  法庭称这一罪行十恶不赦，毫无借口可言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nefarious</td><td>/nɪˈferiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，邪恶的</td><td>nefarious schemes<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators uncovered nefarious schemes to divert relief money into private accounts.  调查人员揭露了把救灾款转入私人账户的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 villainous</td><td>/ˈvɪlənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的，恶劣的</td><td>a villainous plot<br>邪恶的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel turns on a villainous plot against the king.  这部小说围绕一个针对国王的邪恶阴谋展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acceptable</td><td>/əkˈseptəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可接受的，令人满意的</td><td>an acceptable compromise<br>可接受的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The revised offer seemed acceptable to both sides.  修改后的提议似乎双方都能接受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bearable</td><td>/ˈberəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可忍受的</td><td>make the pain bearable<br>使疼痛可以忍受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music made the long night shift almost bearable.  音乐让漫长的夜班几乎可以忍受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 endurable</td><td>/ɛnˈdʊrəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可忍受的，撑得住的</td><td>barely endurable heat<br>几乎无法忍受的酷热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The desert heat was endurable only in the early morning.  沙漠的酷热只有清晨才能忍受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tolerable</td><td>/ˈtɑːlərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可容忍的；尚可的</td><td>a tolerable level of noise<br>可容忍的噪音水平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Conditions in the shelter were crowded but tolerable.  避难所里拥挤，但还能忍受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ate</td><td>/ˈeɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>吃（eat 的过去式）</td><td>ate a light lunch<br>吃了顿简餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We ate a light lunch before the long afternoon hike.  下午长途徒步前我们吃了顿简餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 benignant</del></td><td>/bɪˈnɪɡnənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a benignant smile<br>慈祥的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old abbot listened with a benignant expression on his face.  老修院长面带慈祥的神情倾听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindhearted</td><td>/ˌkaɪndˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好心的，仁慈的</td><td>a kindhearted neighbor<br>好心的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A kindhearted stranger paid for the child's bus ticket.  一位好心的陌生人替那孩子付了车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humane</td><td>/hjuˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>人道的，仁慈的</td><td>humane treatment of prisoners<br>人道地对待囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Activists demanded more humane conditions inside the detention center.  活动人士要求改善拘留中心内的人道条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sympathetic</td><td>/ˌsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同情的，有同情心的</td><td>a sympathetic listener<br>富有同情心的倾听者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse gave her a sympathetic look and held her hand.  护士同情地看着她，握住了她的手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tenderhearted</td><td>/ˌtɛndərˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>心软的，慈悲的</td><td>a tenderhearted child<br>心软的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too tenderhearted to kill the mouse, he released it outside.  他心太软，不忍杀老鼠，便把它放到屋外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>orient</b></td><td>/ˈɔːriˌent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使确定方向，使熟悉或适应</td><td>orient oneself to the city<br>熟悉这座城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New employees need a week to orient themselves to the company culture.  新员工需要一周时间来熟悉公司文化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accustom</td><td>/əˈkʌstəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使习惯</td><td>accustom oneself to cold<br>使自己适应寒冷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had to accustom himself to the cold Boston winters very quickly.  他必须很快让自己习惯波士顿寒冷的冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 familiarize</td><td>/fəˈmɪliəraɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使熟悉，使了解</td><td>familiarize yourself with the rules<br>让自己熟悉规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New staff must familiarize themselves with safety procedures.  新员工必须熟悉安全流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 initiate</td><td>/ɪˈnɪʃieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>创始，发起</td><td>initiate a reform program<br>发起一项改革计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new governor plans to initiate a sweeping reform of rural education.  新州长计划发起一项农村教育的全面改革。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 introduce</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引入，开始实行；介绍</td><td>introduce a new policy<br>推行一项新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city introduced free bus travel for elderly residents.  该市开始为老年居民提供免费公交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 orientate</td><td>/ˈɔriɛnˌteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使确定方向；使适应</td><td>orientate new students<br>帮新生熟悉环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guide helped us orientate ourselves with a paper map.  导游用纸质地图帮我们确定了方位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confuse</td><td>/kənˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；混淆</td><td>confuse the issue<br>混淆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague instructions confused nearly everyone in the training class.  含糊的说明让培训班几乎所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>original</b></td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingenious</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有创造才能的</td><td>an ingenious solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She devised an ingenious method for storing rainwater during the dry season.  她想出了一个在旱季储存雨水的巧妙方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innovative</td><td>/ˈɪnəveɪtɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新性的</td><td>innovative design<br>创新的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm survived the crisis thanks to an innovative approach to distribution.  这家公司靠创新的分销方式度过了危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inventive</td><td>/ɪnˈventɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有创造力的，善发明的</td><td>an inventive chef<br>有创意的厨师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers found an inventive way to recycle factory waste heat.  工程师找到了回收工厂余热的创新办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 earliest</td><td>/ˈɜːrliəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最早的，最先的</td><td>at the earliest opportunity<br>一有机会就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The earliest records of the town date from the twelfth century.  这座城镇最早的记录始于十二世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foremost</td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌmoʊst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>最前的；最重要的</td><td>the foremost expert<br>最权威的专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is the foremost authority on medieval stained glass.  她是中世纪彩色玻璃领域最权威的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 headmost</del></td><td>/ˈhɛdˌmoʊst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>最前面的</td><td>the headmost ship<br>最前面的那条船</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headmost carriage of the train was almost empty.  列车最前面的车厢几乎空着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inaugural</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːɡjərəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>就职的；首次的</td><td>an inaugural address<br>就职演说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's inaugural concert sold out within hours.  该乐团的首场音乐会数小时内售罄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 initial</td><td>/ˌɪˈnɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最初的，开始的</td><td>the initial reaction<br>最初的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our initial estimate turned out to be far too low.  我们最初的估计结果远远偏低。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leadoff</td><td>/ˈliːdˌɔːf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>开头的，居首的</td><td>the leadoff batter<br>首位击球手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His leadoff question set the tone for the whole interview.  他开场的提问定下了整场访谈的调子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maiden</td><td>/ˈmeɪdən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>首次的，初次的</td><td>her maiden voyage<br>处女航</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ship sank on its maiden voyage across the Atlantic.  这艘船在首次横渡大西洋时沉没。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pioneer</td><td>/ˌpaɪəˈnɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>拓荒者，先驱者；开创，创造</td><td>a pioneer in genetics<br>遗传学的先驱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a pioneer in genetics long before the field became fashionable.  在遗传学成为热门之前很久，她就是这一领域的先驱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 premier</td><td>/preˈmɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>首要的，第一流的</td><td>a premier research center<br>首屈一指的研究中心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cambridge remains a premier center for cancer research.  剑桥仍是癌症研究的首要中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virgin</td><td>/ˈvɜːrdʒɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>未开发的，原始的</td><td>virgin forest<br>原始森林</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loggers finally reached the last virgin forest in the valley.  伐木者终于抵达谷中最后一片原始森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 familiar</td><td>/fəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>familiar with the subject<br>熟悉这门学科</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice sounded strangely familiar over the phone.  她的声音在电话里听起来奇怪地熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 time-honored</td><td>/ˈtaɪm ˌɑnərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>历史悠久的，因古老而受尊重的</td><td>a time-honored custom<br>历史悠久的习俗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They opened the harvest with a time-honored village custom.  他们以历史悠久的村俗开始收获季。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tired</td><td>/ˈtaɪərd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疲倦的；厌倦的</td><td>tired of waiting<br>厌倦了等待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By midnight everyone was too tired to argue further.  到午夜时，所有人都累得无法再争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warmed-over</td><td>/ˌwɔrmd ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，老调重弹的</td><td>warmed-over arguments<br>老调重弹的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered only warmed-over arguments from last year.  这篇讲话只是去年论点的老调重弹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 final</td><td>/ˈfaɪnəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最后的，最终的</td><td>the final decision<br>最终决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter explains why the theory was abandoned.  最后一章解释了该理论为何被放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 last</td><td>/ˈlæst/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>最后的；上一个的</td><td>the last remaining copy<br>仅存的最后一本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was the last fluent speaker of that dying language.  他是那种濒危语言最后一位流利使用者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 latter</td><td>/ˈlætər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后者的；后一半的</td><td>the latter half<br>后半段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Of the two plans, the latter proved far cheaper.  两个方案中，后者便宜得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terminal</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>晚期的，致命的</td><td>a terminal illness<br>不治之症</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic specializes in caring for terminal patients.  这家诊所专门照护晚期病人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ultimate</td><td>/ˈʌltəmət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最终的；根本的</td><td>the ultimate goal<br>最终目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Independence was the ultimate aim of their long struggle.  独立是他们长期斗争的最终目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>orthodox</b></td><td>/ˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的</td><td>orthodox medical treatment<br>传统医疗手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many doctors still prefer orthodox treatments to these unproven herbal remedies.  许多医生仍偏爱传统疗法，而非这些未经验证的草药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ceremonial</td><td>/ˌserəˈmoʊniəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>礼仪的，仪式性的</td><td>ceremonial dress<br>礼服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king wore ceremonial robes for the annual procession.  国王为年度游行穿上礼袍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 routine</td><td>/ruˈtiːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>例行的；常规程序</td><td>a routine check<br>例行检查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The problem was discovered during a routine inspection last week.  这个问题是上周例行检查时发现的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casual</td><td>/ˈkæʒəwəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的，非故意的</td><td>a casual remark<br>随口的一句话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A casual remark at dinner accidentally revealed the whole secret.  晚餐时随口一句话意外揭出了整个秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 freewheeling</td><td>/ˈfriːˌwiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随心所欲的，不受拘束的</td><td>a freewheeling discussion<br>无拘无束的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His freewheeling style annoyed the more orthodox critics.  他随心所欲的风格惹恼了较正统的批评家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 informal</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正式的，随意的</td><td>an informal meeting<br>非正式会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute through informal talks over coffee.  他们在咖啡桌旁通过非正式交谈解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irregular</td><td>/ɪˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规则的，不平整的</td><td>an irregular shape<br>不规则的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was made of irregular stones of many sizes.  这条小路由各种大小不规则的石头铺成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unceremonious</td><td>/ˌʌnserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，草率的</td><td>an unceremonious dismissal<br>唐突的解职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unceremonious exit surprised the guests still waiting for dessert.  他唐突离场，让还等着甜点的客人吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unconventional</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非传统的，不合常规的</td><td>an unconventional method<br>非常规的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unconventional teaching style made the class come alive.  她非传统的教学风格让课堂活了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unorthodox</td><td>/ʌnˈɔːrθədɑːks/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正统的，异端的</td><td>an unorthodox approach<br>非正统的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach used unorthodox drills that somehow worked very well.  教练用了非正统的训练，效果却出奇地好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>oscillate</b></td><td>/ˈɑːsɪleɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摆，摆动；犹豫不定</td><td>oscillate between two views<br>在两种观点间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public opinion oscillated wildly during the long campaign.  漫长的竞选期间民意剧烈摇摆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>ossify</b></del></td><td>/ˈɑːsɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）硬化，（使）僵化</td><td>ossify into dogma<br>僵化为教条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Over the decades the bold vision began to ossify into rigid dogma.  几十年间，那个大胆的愿景开始僵化为死板的教条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 make pliant</td><td>/meɪk ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使柔韧，使易弯曲</td><td>make pliant with steam<br>用蒸汽使之柔韧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Steam is used to make the wood for violin backs pliant.  蒸汽被用来让做琴背的木料变柔韧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 transcend conventions</td><td>/trænˈsɛnd kənˈvɛnʃənz/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>超越常规</td><td>transcend conventions boldly<br>大胆超越常规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Great artists transcend conventions rather than merely break them.  伟大的艺术家超越常规，而不只是打破常规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amenable to change</td><td>/əˈminəbəl tu tʃeɪndʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愿意变通的，易于改变的</td><td>remain amenable to change<br>始终乐于改变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A healthy institution stays amenable to change over time.  健康的机构会长期保持乐于变革。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ostentatious</b></td><td>/ˌɑːstenˈteɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>炫耀卖弄的</td><td>ostentatious display of wealth<br>炫富的排场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ostentatious diamond watch drew stares from everyone in the modest room.  他炫耀的钻石表引来了朴素房间里所有人的注视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flaring</td><td>/ˈflerɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>耀眼张扬的；摇曳燃烧的</td><td>flaring colors<br>耀眼的色彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's flaring costume drew every eye in the theater.  舞者张扬耀眼的服装吸引了剧场里所有的目光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flashy</td><td>/ˈflæʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮华的，华而不实的</td><td>a flashy sports car<br>浮华的跑车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a flashy suit that everyone immediately noticed.  他穿着一身浮华的西装到场，所有人立刻都注意到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splashy</td><td>/ˈsplæʃi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，招眼的</td><td>a splashy advertisement<br>一则招眼的广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine ran a splashy cover story about the singer.  杂志刊出一篇关于那位歌手的招眼封面报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpretentious</td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不炫耀的，朴实低调的</td><td>an unpretentious restaurant<br>朴实的小餐馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They live in a small, unpretentious house near the river.  他们住在河边一间朴实的小房子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conservative</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrvətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>守旧的，不愿改变的；不招摇的，低调的</td><td>a conservative estimate<br>保守的估计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents were too conservative to accept such an unconventional career choice.  她的父母太守旧，无法接受这样不合常规的职业选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understated</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含蓄的，不张扬的</td><td>understated elegance<br>含蓄的优雅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her understated gray suit suited the solemn occasion perfectly.  她那身低调的灰西装非常适合这个庄重的场合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unflamboyant</del></td><td>/ˌʌnflæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不张扬的，朴素的</td><td>an unflamboyant style<br>不张扬的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unflamboyant suits contrasted with his loud opinions.  他朴素的西装与他张扬的观点形成对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unflashy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈflæʃi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不炫耀的，低调的</td><td>unflashy but effective<br>低调却有效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She favored unflashy clothes and quiet neighborhood restaurants.  她偏爱低调的衣着和安静的街边餐馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ostracize</b></td><td>/ˈɑːstrəsaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>排斥；放逐；使受冷遇</td><td>ostracize the whistleblower<br>排斥举报者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues ostracized him after he reported the fraud.  他举报欺诈后被同事排斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banish</td><td>/ˈbænɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐出境</td><td>banish him from court<br>将他逐出宫廷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king decided to banish the poet from the court forever.  国王决定把这位诗人永远逐出宫廷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bounce</td><td>/ˈbaʊns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>驱逐，开除；弹跳</td><td>bounce him from the club<br>把他逐出俱乐部</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager bounced two waiters for stealing tips.  经理因偷小费开除了两名侍者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chase</td><td>/tʃeɪs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>驱赶</td><td>chase away<br>赶走，驱散</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer chased the crows away from his young corn.  农夫把乌鸦从他的嫩玉米地里赶走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismiss</td><td>/dɪˈsmɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解散，打发走；解雇；不予考虑</td><td>dismiss the class<br>宣布下课</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher dismissed the class ten minutes ahead of time.  老师提前十分钟宣布下课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expel</td><td>/ɪkˈspel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐；开除</td><td>expel a student<br>开除一名学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school expelled two students for cheating on the exam.  学校因考试作弊开除了两名学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extrude</td><td>/ɪkˈstruːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赶出，逐出；挤压出</td><td>extrude molten metal<br>挤压出熔融金属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The machine extrudes hot plastic into long, even tubes.  机器把热塑料挤压成长而均匀的管子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oust</td><td>/aʊst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驱逐</td><td>oust the corrupt mayor<br>赶走腐败的市长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board voted to oust the chairman after the scandal became public.  丑闻曝光后，董事会投票罢免了主席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boot out</td><td>/buːt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>驱逐，开除</td><td>boot out the manager<br>赶走那位经理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Members voted to boot out the treasurer who lost the funds.  会员投票赶走了亏掉资金的财务主管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cast out</td><td>/ˌkæst ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>驱逐，赶走</td><td>cast out the intruder<br>把入侵者赶出去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village cast out anyone suspected of stealing from the granary.  村里驱逐任何被怀疑偷粮仓的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 runoff</td><td>/ˈrʌnɔːf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赶走，驱逐；决胜选举</td><td>a runoff election<br>决胜选举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two leading candidates met again in a runoff.  两位领先的候选人在决胜选举中再度交锋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drum out</td><td>/drʌm ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>开除，逐出（组织）</td><td>drum out of the party<br>被开除出党</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was drummed out of the regiment for repeated insubordination.  他因屡次抗命被逐出部队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kick out</td><td>/ˌkɪk ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>赶出，开除</td><td>kick out a member<br>开除一名成员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club kicked out two members for repeatedly breaking its rules.  俱乐部因两名成员屡次违规而将其开除。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 throw</td><td>/ˈθroʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>扔，投；（与 out 连用）逐出</td><td>throw out the intruder<br>把闯入者扔出去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guards threw the heckler out of the crowded hall.  保安把那个捣乱者扔出了拥挤的大厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 out</td><td>/ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>副词、介词、形容词</td><td>出去，在外；被逐出的</td><td>throw someone out<br>把某人赶出去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted him out soon after the scandal broke.  丑闻爆发后不久委员会就投票把他赶了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turf out</td><td>/tɜrf ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>赶走，逐出</td><td>turf out the squatters<br>赶走擅自占屋者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council moved to turf out families living in the derelict block.  议会着手赶走住在废弃楼里的家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 take in</td><td>/teɪk ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗；收留；领会</td><td>be taken in by<br>被…欺骗</td></tr>

</tbody>

<tr><td>↳反 include</td><td>/ɪnˈkluːd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>包含，包括</td><td>include all the costs<br>包含全部费用</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embrace</td><td>/ɪmˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>乐于接受；拥抱</td><td>embrace change<br>乐于接受变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young workers quickly embraced the new technology.  年轻员工迅速接受了这项新技术。</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turf out</td><td>/tɜrf ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>赶走，逐出</td><td>turf out the squatters<br>赶走擅自占屋者</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turn out</td><td>/tɜrn ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>赶出，驱逐；结果是</td><td>turn out the tenants<br>把房客赶出去</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>overweening</b></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 assumptive</del></td><td>/əˈsʌmptɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自以为是的；假定的</td><td>an assumptive attitude<br>傲慢的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His assumptive manner annoyed everyone at the negotiating table.  他傲慢的态度让谈判桌上的人都很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presuming</td><td>/prɪˈzuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自以为是的，冒失放肆的</td><td>a presuming stranger<br>冒失的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was presuming of him to offer advice uninvited.  他未经邀请就给建议，实在冒失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretentious</td><td>/prɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，自命不凡的</td><td>pretentious prose<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pretentious speech annoyed almost everyone in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲让几乎所有听众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>outlandish</b></td><td>/aʊtˈlændɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇异的</td><td>outlandish conspiracy theories<br>离奇的阴谋论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her outlandish hat made every head turn as she entered the hall.  她走进大厅时，那顶古怪的帽子让所有人回头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
