<tr><td>↳近 bizarre</td><td>/bəˈzɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怪异的，离奇的</td><td>a bizarre coincidence<br>离奇的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By some bizarre coincidence we wore the same coat.  出于某种离奇的巧合，我们穿了同款外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cranky</td><td>/ˈkræŋki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>脾气坏的，易怒的</td><td>a cranky toddler<br>闹脾气的幼儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The baby was cranky all night because of the heat.  因为天热，宝宝整夜都在闹脾气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eccentric</td><td>/ɪkˈsentrɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>古怪的，不循规蹈矩的</td><td>an eccentric old professor<br>行为古怪的老教授</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An eccentric old professor kept three clocks that all showed different times.  一位行为古怪的老教授留着三只指着不同时间的钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 erratic</td><td>/ɪˈrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，不规律的</td><td>erratic driving behavior<br>飘忽不定的驾驶行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His erratic driving behavior alarmed the passengers long before the police stopped him.  早在警察拦下他之前，他飘忽不定的驾驶行为就让乘客惊慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 offbeat</td><td>/ˌɔːfˈbiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平常的</td><td>an offbeat sense of humor<br>别致的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offbeat sense of humor won over an audience that had arrived skeptical.  他别致的幽默感赢得了原本抱着怀疑到场的观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remarkable</td><td>/rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，引人注目的</td><td>a remarkable achievement<br>非凡的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Finishing the bridge in a year was a remarkable achievement.  一年建成这座桥是非凡的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 familiar</td><td>/fəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>familiar with the subject<br>熟悉这门学科</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice sounded strangely familiar over the phone.  她的声音在电话里听起来奇怪地熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonexotic</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnɪɡˈzɑtɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不异域的，平常的</td><td>nonexotic local plants<br>非外来的本地植物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden used only nonexotic species native to the region.  这座花园只用本地原生的非外来物种。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonglamorous</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈɡlæmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不迷人的，平淡无奇的</td><td>nonglamorous daily work<br>平淡无奇的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most research is nonglamorous work involving spreadsheets and long quiet afternoons.  多数研究是涉及表格和漫长安静下午的平淡工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain-jane</td><td>/ˌpleɪn ˈdʒeɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡无奇的，不起眼的</td><td>a plain-jane design<br>朴素平淡的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drove a plain-jane sedan that nobody ever looked at twice.  她开一辆没人会多看一眼的普通轿车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unglamorous</td><td>/ʌnˈɡlæmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不迷人的，平淡无奇的</td><td>unglamorous but useful work<br>不光鲜但有用的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most research is unglamorous, patient work in a quiet lab.  多数研究是安静实验室里不光鲜的耐心工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unromantic</td><td>/ˌʌnroʊˈmæntɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不浪漫的，平实的</td><td>an unromantic account<br>平实的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unromantic account of the voyage listed only costs and dates.  他对航行的平实叙述只列了费用和日期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>outmaneuver</b></td><td>/ˌaʊtməˈnuːvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>以策略取胜，智胜</td><td>outmaneuver the opposition<br>在策略上胜过反对派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She outmaneuvered older rivals to win the committee's support.  她在策略上胜过资深对手，赢得委员会支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outfox</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈfɑːks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>以智取胜，比…更聪明</td><td>outfox the competition<br>智胜竞争对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small studio outfoxed the giants with one clever release.  这家小工作室凭一次巧妙发行智胜巨头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outslick</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈslɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用手段胜过，智胜</td><td>outslick a rival<br>耍手段胜过对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to outslick the negotiators and lost the contract.  他想耍手段胜过谈判方，结果丢了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outsmart</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>比…更聪明，智胜</td><td>outsmart the police<br>智胜警方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thieves outsmarted every alarm in the museum that night.  那晚窃贼智胜了博物馆的所有警报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outthink</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈθɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>在思考上胜过，谋略上超越</td><td>outthink the opponent<br>在谋略上胜过对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A patient player can outthink a faster but reckless rival.  沉稳的选手能在谋略上胜过更快却莽撞的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outwit</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈwɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>智胜；以智谋取胜</td><td>outwit the guards<br>智胜守卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clever fox managed to outwit the hunters and slip into the forest.  聪明的狐狸智胜了猎人，溜进了森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yield</td><td>/ˈjiːld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>产生，出产；屈服，让出</td><td>yield results<br>产生结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of patient testing finally yielded a usable vaccine.  数月耐心的试验终于产出可用的疫苗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>outset</b></td><td>/ˈaʊtset/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开端，开始</td><td>from the outset<br>从一开始</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the outset, she made it clear that she would not compromise.  从一开始，她就明确表示自己不会妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alpha</td><td>/ˈælfə/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>开端，第一个；最强者</td><td>the alpha and omega<br>始与终</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Curiosity was the alpha of his entire scientific career.  好奇心是他整个科学生涯的开端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commencement</td><td>/kəˈmensmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开始，开端；毕业典礼</td><td>the commencement of talks<br>谈判的开始</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The commencement of construction was delayed by heavy spring rains.  施工的开始因春季大雨而延后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genesis</td><td>/ˈdʒenəsɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>起源，创始</td><td>the genesis of the crisis<br>危机的起源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The genesis of the movement lies in a single student protest.  这场运动的起源是一次学生抗议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inception</td><td>/ɪnˈsepʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>开端，开始</td><td>since its inception<br>自创立以来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club has doubled in size since its inception three years ago.  这个俱乐部自三年前创立以来规模翻了一倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 incipience</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsɪpiəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开始，初期阶段</td><td>at its incipience<br>在其初始阶段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disease is easiest to treat at its incipience.  这种病在初期阶段最容易治疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nascence</del></td><td>/ˈneɪsəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诞生，发生，起源</td><td>the nascence of a movement<br>一场运动的诞生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Those pamphlets document the nascence of the labor movement.  那些小册子记录了工人运动的诞生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 termination</td><td>/tərməˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>终止，结束</td><td>the termination of the contract<br>合同的终止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Either side may request termination of the agreement early.  任何一方都可要求提前终止协议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 close</td><td>/kloʊz/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>关闭；结束</td><td>close the meeting<br>结束会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They closed the discussion before anyone could object.  他们在有人反对之前结束了讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conclusion</td><td>/kənˈkluːʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>结束；结论</td><td>bring to a conclusion<br>使结束</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial reached its conclusion after eleven long weeks.  审判在漫长的十一周后结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 end</td><td>/ˈend/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>结束；末端</td><td>end the war<br>结束战争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strike ended after both sides agreed on wages.  双方就工资达成一致后罢工结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ending</td><td>/ˈendɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>结局，结尾</td><td>a happy ending<br>幸福的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel has an ending that nobody sees coming.  这部小说的结局谁都想不到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 omega</td><td>/oʊˈmeɡə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>结尾，最后</td><td>the alpha and omega<br>始与终</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For him, that discovery was the omega of a lifelong search.  对他而言，那个发现是毕生求索的终点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>outwit</b></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈwɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>智胜；以智谋取胜</td><td>outwit the guards<br>智胜守卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clever fox managed to outwit the hunters and slip into the forest.  聪明的狐狸智胜了猎人，溜进了森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outfox</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈfɑːks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>以智取胜，比…更聪明</td><td>outfox the competition<br>智胜竞争对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small studio outfoxed the giants with one clever release.  这家小工作室凭一次巧妙发行智胜巨头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outmaneuver</td><td>/ˌaʊtməˈnuːvər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>以策略取胜，智胜</td><td>outmaneuver the opposition<br>在策略上胜过反对派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She outmaneuvered older rivals to win the committee's support.  她在策略上胜过资深对手，赢得委员会支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outslick</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈslɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用手段胜过，智胜</td><td>outslick a rival<br>耍手段胜过对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to outslick the negotiators and lost the contract.  他想耍手段胜过谈判方，结果丢了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outsmart</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>比…更聪明，智胜</td><td>outsmart the police<br>智胜警方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thieves outsmarted every alarm in the museum that night.  那晚窃贼智胜了博物馆的所有警报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outthink</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈθɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>在思考上胜过，谋略上超越</td><td>outthink the opponent<br>在谋略上胜过对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A patient player can outthink a faster but reckless rival.  沉稳的选手能在谋略上胜过更快却莽撞的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>overbearing</b></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈberɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的</td><td>an overbearing boss<br>专横傲慢的老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overbearing manner made junior colleagues afraid to voice any objection.  他专横的作风让年轻同事不敢提出任何反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authoritative</td><td>/əˈθɔːrəˌteɪtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专断的，命令式的；权威的</td><td>an authoritative tone<br>专断的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She silenced the room with one authoritative wave of her hand.  她专断地一挥手就让全场安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despotic</td><td>/dɪˈspɑːtɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专制的，暴虐的</td><td>a despotic regime<br>专制的政权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country suffered under a despotic regime for decades.  这个国家在专制政权下受苦数十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dictatorial</td><td>/ˌdɪktəˈtɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>独裁的；专横的</td><td>a dictatorial manner<br>专横的作风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach's dictatorial manner made several players quit.  教练专横的态度让几名球员退出了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 masterful</td><td>/ˈmæstərfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺精湛的，娴熟的</td><td>a masterful performance<br>一场技艺精湛的演出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her masterful handling of the violin silenced the whole hall.  她精湛的小提琴技艺让全场肃静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meek</td><td>/miːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的</td><td>a meek and quiet manner<br>温顺安静的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too meek to complain, he accepted the extra work without saying a word.  他太顺从了，不敢抱怨，一句话没说就接下了额外的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unassuming</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈsuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦逊的，不张扬的</td><td>quiet and unassuming<br>安静谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The famous author was surprisingly unassuming in person.  这位著名作家本人出乎意料地谦逊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>overlap</b></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈlæp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（与…）部分重叠</td><td>overlap in some areas<br>在某些领域重叠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our two research projects overlap in several important areas.  我们两个研究项目在几个重要领域有重叠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lap</td><td>/ˈlæp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>重叠，搭盖；一圈</td><td>lap one tile over another<br>让瓦片彼此叠盖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The roofer lapped each shingle over the one below it.  屋顶工让每块瓦叠盖住下面那块。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlay</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈleɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>覆盖在…上；覆盖层</td><td>overlay the old map<br>叠加在旧地图上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers overlaid the old chart with new survey data.  工程师把新测量数据叠加在旧图上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overlie</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈlaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>位于…之上，覆盖</td><td>overlie the bedrock<br>覆盖在基岩之上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick clay deposits overlie the limestone across the whole plain.  厚厚的黏土层覆盖着整片平原的石灰岩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overspread</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈsprɛd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>铺满，蔓延于</td><td>overspread the valley<br>铺满整个山谷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mist overspread the valley before the sun rose.  日出前雾气铺满了整个山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>overt</b></td><td>/oʊˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，公开的</td><td>overt hostility<br>公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The policy met overt hostility from local business owners.  该政策遭到当地商户的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shadowy</td><td>/ˈʃædoʊi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐秘的，模糊不清的</td><td>a shadowy organization<br>隐秘的组织</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Money reached the party through a shadowy network of donors.  资金通过隐秘的捐款人网络流向该党。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>overture</b></td><td>/ˈoʊvərtʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序曲，前奏；提议</td><td>play the overture<br>演奏序曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra opened the evening with a lively overture by Rossini.  乐团以罗西尼一首轻快的序曲开始了晚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preamble</td><td>/ˈpriːæmbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>序言，开场白</td><td>the preamble to the treaty<br>条约的序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preamble to the treaty states its guiding principles.  条约的序言阐明了其指导原则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preliminary</td><td>/prɪˈlɪməˌneri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>初步的；预备阶段</td><td>preliminary findings<br>初步结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Preliminary results suggested the drug slowed the disease.  初步结果显示该药延缓了病情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prologue</td><td>/ˈproʊlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序言</td><td>the prologue to the play<br>该剧的序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prologue to the play explains how the bitter feud began.  该剧的序言说明了这场激烈仇怨的由来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warm-up</td><td>/ˈwɔrm ʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热身，预热活动</td><td>a short warm-up act<br>简短的开场表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band played a short warm-up before the headline set.  乐队在主场演出前做了简短的热身表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curtain-raiser</td><td>/ˈkɜrtən ˌreɪzər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开场戏，序幕活动</td><td>a curtain-raiser to the festival<br>节庆的开幕活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The parade served as a curtain-raiser to the summer festival.  游行成了夏季节庆的开幕活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coda</td><td>/ˈkoʊdə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>终曲</td><td>a triumphant coda<br>辉煌的终曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The symphony ends with a quiet and unexpectedly gentle coda.  这部交响曲以安静而出乎意料温柔的尾声结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>oxymoron</b></td><td>/ˌɑːksɪˈmɔːrɑːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>矛盾修饰法</td><td>a deliberate oxymoron<br>刻意的矛盾修饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The phrase deafening silence is a famous oxymoron used by many poets.  「震耳欲聋的沉默」是许多诗人用过的著名矛盾修饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pacify</b></td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rankle</td><td>/ˈræŋkl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>rankle in his mind<br>在他心里作痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unfair remark continued to rankle him for many years afterward.  那句不公平的评论此后多年一直令他愤懑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tantrum</td><td>/ˈtæntrəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勃然大怒，发脾气</td><td>throw a tantrum<br>大发脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired child threw a tantrum in the middle of the store.  疲倦的孩子在商店中央大发脾气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 discompose</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安；使混乱</td><td>discompose the audience<br>让观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing seemed to discompose the veteran pilot, not even engine failure.  似乎没什么能让这位老飞行员心慌，连引擎故障也不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>padding</b></td><td>/ˈpædɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张，废话</td><td>cut out the padding<br>删掉那些废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay would be much stronger without so much padding in the middle.  这篇文章若去掉中间那么多废话会有力得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caricature</td><td>/ˈkærɪkətʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>讽刺画，夸张的滑稽模仿</td><td>draw a caricature<br>画一幅讽刺画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cartoonist drew a cruel caricature of the new mayor.  漫画家画了一幅刻薄的新市长讽刺画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coloring</td><td>/ˈkʌlərɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张渲染；着色</td><td>add some coloring<br>添些夸张渲染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account had enough coloring to make the fight sound heroic.  他的叙述加了足够渲染，把打斗说得很英勇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elaboration</td><td>/ɪˌlæbəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>详加阐述；添枝加叶</td><td>need further elaboration<br>需要进一步阐述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal was accepted after some elaboration of the budget.  预算经一番详述后，提案获得通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embellishment</td><td>/emˈbelɪʃmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张修饰，渲染</td><td>harmless embellishment<br>无害的渲染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tells the story with a little embellishment each time.  他每次讲这故事都添一点渲染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embroidery</td><td>/ɪmˈbrɔɪdəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>渲染夸大；刺绣</td><td>embroidery of the facts<br>对事实的渲染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His memoir contains obvious embroidery of a modest career.  他的回忆录对平凡的经历明显有所渲染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hyperbole</td><td>/haɪˈpɜːrbəli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张</td><td>obvious hyperbole<br>明显的夸张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His claim of a million loyal fans is obvious hyperbole.  他声称拥有百万忠实粉丝，这显然是夸张之辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnification</td><td>/ˌmæɡnəfəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大，放大</td><td>magnification of the danger<br>对危险的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report's magnification of small risks alarmed the whole town.  报告对小风险的夸大惊动了全镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overstatement</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌsteɪtmənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大陈述，言过其实</td><td>a slight overstatement<br>略微的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Calling it the best film ever is a clear overstatement.  说它是史上最佳影片显然言过其实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stretching</td><td>/ˈstretʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大，拉长；伸展</td><td>a stretching of the truth<br>对事实的夸大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Calling that a discovery is a stretching of the term.  把那称为发现，是对这个词的夸大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meiosis</td><td>/maɪˈoʊsɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>含蓄陈述法；减数分裂</td><td>use meiosis for effect<br>用含蓄陈述取效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Calling the storm a shower is meiosis, not honesty.  把风暴说成小雨是含蓄陈述，而非诚实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understatement</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>保守的说法，轻描淡写</td><td>a masterpiece of understatement<br>轻描淡写的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying the room was messy is a serious understatement.  说这房间乱，实在是太保守了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>paean</b></td><td>/ˈpiːən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐颂，赞扬</td><td>a paean to freedom<br>对自由的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review read like a paean to the young violinist and her talent.  那篇评论读来像是对这位年轻小提琴手及其才华的赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accolade</td><td>/ˈækəleɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>赞扬，荣誉；奖励</td><td>win the highest accolade<br>赢得最高荣誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel received accolades from critics on both continents.  这部小说获得了两大洲评论家的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 citation</td><td>/saɪˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>表彰，嘉奖；引文</td><td>a citation for bravery<br>英勇嘉奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She received a citation for bravery after the rescue.  救援之后她获得了英勇嘉奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dithyramb</del></td><td>/ˈdɪθəræm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热情洋溢的赞歌</td><td>a dithyramb to spring<br>献给春天的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His review was less an essay than a dithyramb to the singer.  他的评论不像文章，更像献给歌手的狂热赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eulogium</del></td><td>/juˈloʊdʒiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>颂词，赞美之辞</td><td>deliver a warm eulogium<br>致热情的颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old professor delivered a graceful eulogium for his student.  老教授为学生致了一篇优雅的颂词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hymn</td><td>/hɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞歌，赞美诗</td><td>sing a hymn<br>唱赞美诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole congregation stood up to sing a hymn of thanksgiving.  全体教众站起身来唱一首感恩的赞美诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tribute</td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>painstaking</b></td><td>/ˈpeɪnzteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>煞费苦心的，一丝不苟的</td><td>painstaking research<br>煞费苦心的研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of painstaking research went into this single short article.  这篇短文背后是多年一丝不苟的研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conscientious</td><td>/ˌkɑːnʃiˈenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仔细的，一丝不苟的；有良心的，正直的</td><td>a conscientious worker<br>一位认真的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A conscientious editor checks every fact before the article goes to print.  一丝不苟的编辑会在文章付印前核实每一个事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fussy</td><td>/ˈfʌsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，过分讲究的</td><td>fussy about small details<br>对小细节很挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tailor is fussy about the smallest details.  这位老裁缝对最小的细节都很挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meticulous</td><td>/məˈtɪkjələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一丝不苟的，极为细致的</td><td>meticulous attention to detail<br>对细节极为在意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restorer's meticulous attention to detail saved the fragile manuscript from further damage.  修复师一丝不苟地关注细节，使这份脆弱的手稿免于进一步损坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrupulous</td><td>/ˈskruːpjələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的；一丝不苟的</td><td>scrupulous honesty<br>一丝不苟的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her scrupulous honesty made her the obvious choice to handle the accounts.  她一丝不苟的诚实使她成为管账的当然人选。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slipshod</td><td>/ˈslɪpʃɑːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，随意的</td><td>slipshod workmanship<br>马虎的做工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inspector criticized the slipshod workmanship on the newly built bridge.  检查员批评新建桥梁马虎的做工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cursory</td><td>/ˈkɜːrsəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的，不注意细节的</td><td>a cursory glance<br>匆匆一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cursory inspection missed the cracks running along the bridge supports.  一次匆忙的检查漏掉了桥墩上的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>palatable</b></td><td>/ˈpælətəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；可接受的</td><td>a palatable compromise<br>令人满意的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef made the healthy vegetables surprisingly palatable to the children.  厨师把健康的蔬菜做得让孩子们意外地喜欢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blessed</td><td>/ˈblesɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>幸福的，有福的</td><td>a blessed relief<br>极大的宽慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the noise, the silence was a blessed relief.  喧闹过后，寂静是极大的宽慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congenial</td><td>/kənˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和善的，友好的</td><td>a congenial host<br>一位和善的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our congenial host made every guest feel completely at home.  我们和善的主人让每位客人都感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delectable</td><td>/dɪˈlektəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；令人愉悦的</td><td>a delectable dessert<br>美味的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a delectable dessert made from summer berries.  她端上用夏日莓果做的美味甜点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 delightsome</del></td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的</td><td>a delightsome garden<br>令人愉悦的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They wandered through a delightsome garden full of roses.  他们漫步在满是玫瑰的怡人花园里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreamy</td><td>/ˈdriːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美妙的；恍惚的</td><td>a dreamy summer evening<br>美妙的夏夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent a dreamy evening beside the quiet lake.  他们在静湖边度过了一个美妙的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dulcet</td><td>/ˈdʌlsɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，令人愉悦的</td><td>dulcet tones<br>悦耳的音调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dulcet tones calmed the restless children within a couple of minutes.  她悦耳的音调几分钟内就安抚了不安的孩子们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enjoyable</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒɔɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，有趣的</td><td>an enjoyable evening<br>愉快的一晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had a thoroughly enjoyable evening at the concert.  我们在音乐会上度过了非常愉快的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 felicitous</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsətəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恰当的，贴切的</td><td>a felicitous phrase<br>贴切的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The title is a felicitous choice for such a book.  这个书名对这样一本书来说是贴切的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grateful</td><td>/ˈɡreɪtfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人舒适的；感激的</td><td>grateful shade at noon<br>正午宜人的树荫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The travelers rested in the grateful shade of an old oak.  旅人们在老橡树宜人的树荫下歇息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gratifying</td><td>/ˈɡrætəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的，令人欣慰的</td><td>a gratifying result<br>令人满意的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was gratifying to see her hard work finally rewarded.  看到她的努力终获回报令人欣慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavenly</td><td>/ˈhevənli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天上的；美妙无比的</td><td>heavenly bodies<br>天体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Astronomers charted the heavenly bodies visible that clear night.  天文学家绘制了那个晴夜可见的天体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jolly</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>欢乐的；开玩笑</td><td>a jolly atmosphere<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jolly innkeeper kept refilling everyone's glass and telling old stories.  这位欢快的店主不停给每人续杯，还讲着旧日的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luscious</td><td>/ˈlʌʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>甘美的，甜美诱人的</td><td>luscious ripe peaches<br>甘美的熟桃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market sold luscious peaches picked that morning.  市场上卖着当天早上采的甘美桃子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pleasurable</td><td>/ˈpleʒərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的</td><td>a pleasurable experience<br>愉快的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reading aloud together became a pleasurable evening habit.  一起朗读成了愉快的晚间习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfying</td><td>/ˈsætɪsˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的；有说服力的</td><td>a satisfying explanation<br>令人满意的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one offered a satisfying answer to the reporter's question.  没人对记者的问题给出令人满意的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disagreeable</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈɡriːəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的；脾气坏的</td><td>a disagreeable task<br>令人不快的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cleaning the drains was a thoroughly disagreeable job.  清理下水道是件极其讨人厌的活儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pleasureless</del></td><td>/ˈplɛʒərləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无乐趣的</td><td>a pleasureless routine<br>毫无乐趣的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of pleasureless work had drained his curiosity.  多年毫无乐趣的工作耗尽了他的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpalatable</td><td>/ʌnˈpælətəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难吃的；令人难以接受的</td><td>an unpalatable truth<br>难以接受的真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report contained several unpalatable truths about spending.  报告里有几条关于开支的难堪真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpleasant</td><td>/ʌnˈplezənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的，讨厌的</td><td>an unpleasant surprise<br>令人不快的意外</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was an unpleasant smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwelcome</td><td>/ʌnˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受欢迎的，讨厌的</td><td>unwelcome news<br>不受欢迎的消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrival brought unwelcome news about the closing factory.  他的到来带来了关于工厂停产的坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>palatial</b></td><td>/pəˈleɪʃl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宫殿般奢华的</td><td>a palatial hotel lobby<br>宫殿般的酒店大堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bank had a palatial marble hall.  那家老银行有一座宫殿般的大理石厅堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deluxe</td><td>/dəˈlʌks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，高级的</td><td>a deluxe suite<br>豪华套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They booked a deluxe suite overlooking the harbor.  他们订了一间可俯瞰港口的豪华套房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luxuriant</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>luxuriant vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Luxuriant ferns covered the damp floor of the forest.  茂盛的蕨类覆盖了潮湿的林地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opulent</td><td>/ˈɑːpjələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富裕的；华丽的</td><td>an opulent hotel lobby<br>豪华的酒店大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opulent ballroom was lined with gold mirrors and velvet chairs.  这间华丽的舞厅摆满了金框镜子和天鹅绒椅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sumptuous</td><td>/ˈsʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，奢侈的</td><td>a sumptuous banquet<br>一场豪华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served a sumptuous banquet for the visiting delegation.  酒店为来访代表团摆了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ascetic</td><td>/əˈsetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>苦行的，禁欲的；苦行者</td><td>an ascetic lifestyle<br>苦修的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk led an ascetic life on bread and cold water.  这位僧人以面包和冷水过着自我克制的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 no-frills</td><td>/ˌnoʊ ˈfrɪlz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不加装饰的，简朴实用的</td><td>a no-frills airline<br>廉价简朴的航空公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They flew a no-frills airline with no meals included.  他们坐了一家不含餐食的简朴廉价航空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spartan</td><td>/ˈspɑːrtn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简朴的，节约的</td><td>spartan living conditions<br>简朴的生活条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks lived in spartan rooms with only a bed and a lamp.  僧侣们住在只有一床一灯的简朴房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pall</b></td><td>/pɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>令人厌倦，失去吸引力</td><td>begin to pall<br>开始令人生厌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelty of city life began to pall after his first lonely winter.  在孤独的第一个冬天过后，城市生活的新鲜感开始让他生厌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interest</td><td>/ˈɪntrəst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>兴趣，关注；利息</td><td>show interest in music<br>对音乐表现出兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She lost interest in the project after only a month.  仅一个月后她就对这个项目失去了兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrigue</td><td>/ˈɪntriːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>阴谋；激起…的兴趣</td><td>political intrigue<br>政治阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Court records reveal decades of political intrigue behind the sudden fall of the minister.  法庭记录揭示了这位大臣突然倒台背后数十年的政治阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>palliate</b></del></td><td>/ˈpælieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓和；掩饰</td><td>palliate the symptoms<br>缓解症状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can only palliate the pain, not cure the disease.  这种新药只能减轻疼痛，无法治愈疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allay</td><td>/əˈleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，平息</td><td>allay public fears<br>平息公众恐慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement was meant to allay concerns about safety.  这份声明意在减轻人们对安全的担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alleviate</td><td>/əˈliːvieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓和，减轻</td><td>alleviate the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A warm bath helped alleviate the soreness in his back.  热水澡帮他缓解了背部的酸痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mitigate</td><td>/ˈmɪtɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，使缓和</td><td>mitigate the damage<br>减轻损害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Planting trees can mitigate the effects of summer heat.  种树可以减轻夏季高温的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relieve</td><td>/rɪˈliːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓解；换班替换</td><td>relieve the pressure<br>缓解压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk relieved the tension in her shoulders.  短暂散步缓解了她肩膀的紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exacerbate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæsərbeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧，使恶化</td><td>exacerbate the crisis<br>使危机加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the budget would only exacerbate the housing shortage.  削减预算只会使住房短缺进一步恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 worsen</td><td>/ˈwɜːrsən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变坏，恶化</td><td>worsen over time<br>随时间恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cough worsened after a week outdoors in the cold.  在寒冷的户外待了一周后，她的咳嗽加重了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 increase intensity</td><td>/ɪnˈkris ɪnˈtɛnsɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>加剧，加强程度</td><td>increase intensity of pain<br>加剧疼痛程度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stopping the medication may increase intensity of the symptoms.  停药可能加剧症状的程度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pallid</b></td><td>/ˈpælɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生气的，缺乏活力的</td><td>a pallid imitation<br>毫无生气的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The remake was a pallid imitation of a far livelier original.  这部翻拍片是对一部活力十足的原作的毫无生气的模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ashen</td><td>/ˈæʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灰白的，苍白的</td><td>an ashen face<br>苍白的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came back from the hospital with an ashen face.  他从医院回来时脸色灰白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ashy</td><td>/ˈæʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灰白的，如灰般苍白的</td><td>ashy pale skin<br>灰白的皮肤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her ashy complexion alarmed everyone at the table.  她灰白的脸色让餐桌上的人都吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanched</td><td>/blæntʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>变白的，发白的</td><td>a blanched face<br>发白的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her blanched face showed how frightened she really was.  她发白的脸显出她真的很害怕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pasty</td><td>/ˈpeɪsti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>苍白的，气色差的</td><td>a pasty complexion<br>苍白的气色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks indoors left him with a pasty complexion.  数周待在室内使他气色苍白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wan</td><td>/wɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>苍白的，病态的</td><td>a wan smile<br>苍白无力的一笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave us a wan smile and went back to bed.  她苍白无力地笑了一下，又回床上躺着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 piquant</td><td>/ˈpiːkənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人振奋的</td><td>a piquant remark<br>令人振奋的妙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His piquant remarks kept the whole table laughing through dinner.  他令人振奋的妙语让整桌人整顿晚餐都在笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>palmy</b></td><td>/ˈpɑːmi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁荣的</td><td>the palmy days of radio<br>广播的繁荣年代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He liked to reminisce about the palmy days of the family business.  他喜欢追忆家族生意繁荣的那些年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flourishing</td><td>/ˈflɜːrɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁荣的，兴旺的</td><td>a flourishing trade<br>兴旺的贸易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The port supported a flourishing trade in spices.  这座港口支撑起兴旺的香料贸易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prosperous</td><td>/ˈprɑːspərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>成功的，繁盛的</td><td>a prosperous merchant town<br>繁盛的商镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harbor grew into a prosperous merchant town within a single generation.  这个港口在一代人的时间里发展成繁盛的商镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thriving</td><td>/ˈθraɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁荣的，兴盛的</td><td>a thriving local economy<br>兴盛的地方经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town has a thriving market every Saturday morning.  这个镇每周六上午都有兴旺的集市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 booming</td><td>/ˈbuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速发展的，繁荣的</td><td>a booming housing market<br>火热的房地产市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A booming tech sector pushed rents beyond most salaries.  科技业的繁荣把租金推高到多数薪水之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lush</td><td>/lʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>lush vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lush vegetation covered the valley floor after weeks of steady rain.  连续数周的降雨之后，茂盛的植被覆盖了谷底。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roaring</td><td>/ˈrɔːrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>咆哮的，轰鸣的</td><td>a roaring engine<br>轰鸣的引擎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We could hardly talk over the roaring waterfall.  在轰鸣的瀑布声中我们几乎无法交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 halcyon</td><td>/ˈhælsiən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宁静的，平静的</td><td>halcyon days<br>宁静美好的日子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He often talked about the halcyon days of his youth.  他常谈起年轻时那些宁静美好的日子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 failed</td><td>/ˈfeɪld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>失败的，破产的</td><td>a failed business venture<br>失败的创业尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He returned home after a failed venture in the capital.  在首都创业失败后他返回家乡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 depressed</td><td>/dɪˈprest/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不开心的，情绪不高的，消沉的</td><td>feel deeply depressed<br>感到十分消沉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt depressed for weeks after the letter of rejection arrived.  收到拒信之后，她消沉了好几个星期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unprosperous</del></td><td>/ʌnˈprɑspərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不景气的，不兴旺的</td><td>an unprosperous region<br>不景气的地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mine's closure left an unprosperous valley behind it.  矿场关闭留下了一个不景气的山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsuccessful</td><td>/ˌʌnsəkˈsesfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不成功的，失败的</td><td>an unsuccessful attempt<br>不成功的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After three unsuccessful attempts, she changed her method.  三次尝试失败后，她改变了方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>palpable</b></td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 apprehensible</del></td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhensəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可理解的</td><td>readily apprehensible ideas<br>易于理解的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument is barely apprehensible to readers without training.  他的论证对没有受过训练的读者几乎难以理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detectable</td><td>/dɪˈtektəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可检测到的，可察觉的</td><td>detectable levels of lead<br>可检测到的铅含量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new sensor finds detectable traces of gas within seconds.  新传感器几秒内就能发现可检测到的气体痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discernible</td><td>/dɪˈsɜːrnəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可辨别的，可看出的</td><td>no discernible pattern<br>看不出规律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was no discernible improvement after three weeks of treatment.  治疗三周后没有可察觉的改善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguishable</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可区分的，可辨别的</td><td>clearly distinguishable from others<br>与其他明显可区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins are barely distinguishable except for a small scar.  这对双胞胎除了一道小疤几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensible</td><td>/ˈsensəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，合理的</td><td>a sensible choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing boots was a sensible choice in that mud.  在那种泥地里穿靴子是明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperceptible</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈseptɪbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>察觉不到的，极微小的</td><td>an imperceptible shift<br>察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in temperature was almost imperceptible to the guests.  客人几乎察觉不到温度的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inappreciable</del></td><td>/ˌɪnəˈpriʃəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的，感觉不到的</td><td>an inappreciable amount<br>微不足道的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk from such a tiny dose is inappreciable.  如此微小剂量带来的风险微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indistinguishable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法区分的，难以辨别的</td><td>indistinguishable from the original<br>与原件无法区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The copy was almost indistinguishable from the original painting.  这份复制品与原画几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensible</td><td>/ɪnˈsensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知觉的，昏迷的</td><td>insensible to pain<br>感觉不到疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blow left him insensible for several minutes.  这一击使他几分钟内失去了知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undetectable</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈtektəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法检测到的，察觉不到的</td><td>undetectable by radar<br>雷达无法探测到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus remained undetectable in blood tests for several weeks.  这种病毒在血液检测中数周都无法检出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>palter</b></del></td><td>/ˈpɔːltər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欺骗；讨价还价</td><td>palter with the truth<br>对真相含糊其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses should not palter with the truth when giving sworn testimony.  证人在宣誓作证时不应对真相含糊其词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 chaffer</del></td><td>/ˈtʃæfər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>讨价还价，磨价</td><td>chaffer over the price<br>为价格讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two traders chaffered over the price for an hour.  两个商人为价格磨了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deal</td><td>/ˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>交易，协议；处理</td><td>close a deal<br>达成交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They finally struck a deal after weeks of tense talks.  经过数周紧张谈判，他们终于达成交易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dicker</td><td>/ˈdɪkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>讨价还价，商议</td><td>dicker over terms<br>就条款讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They dickered over delivery terms until both sides tired.  他们就交货条款争到双方都累了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haggle</td><td>/ˈhæɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>讨价还价，争论不休</td><td>haggle over the price<br>就价格讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists haggle with vendors over the price of every souvenir.  游客与摊贩为每件纪念品讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horse-trade</td><td>/ˈhɔrs ˌtreɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>讨价还价，互相让步交易</td><td>horse-trade for votes<br>为选票讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Senators horse-traded for weeks before the bill finally passed.  参议员们讨价还价数周，法案才终获通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negotiate</td><td>/nɪˈɡoʊʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>商量，谈判</td><td>negotiate a contract<br>谈判一份合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides negotiated a contract that satisfied neither of them completely.  双方谈成了一份合同，但双方都不完全满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocate</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（带有欺骗目的地）模棱两可地说，说谎话</td><td>equivocate under questioning<br>受问时含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She equivocated under questioning about the true state of her finances.  在被问及财务真实状况时，她含糊其辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 candor</td><td>/ˈkændər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坦率，直言不讳</td><td>speak with candor<br>坦率地讲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I appreciated her candor about the risks of the operation.  我很欣赏她坦率说明手术的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>paltry</b></td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggling</td><td>/ˈnɪɡəlɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的；恼人琐碎的</td><td>a niggling doubt<br>一丝挥不去的疑虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A niggling doubt about the figures kept him awake.  对那些数字的一丝疑虑让他难以入睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 piddling</td><td>/ˈpɪdlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>a piddling sum of money<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a piddling sum for months of demanding work.  他们为数月繁重的工作只给了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 piffling</del></td><td>/ˈpɪflɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>琐碎的，微不足道的</td><td>piffling objections<br>无关紧要的反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee wasted hours on piffling procedural questions.  委员会在琐碎的程序问题上浪费了数小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 big</td><td>/ˈbɪɡ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，大幅的；重要的</td><td>a big salary increase<br>大幅加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was offered a big raise and took the job at once.  她获得大幅加薪，立刻接受了这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pan</b></td><td>/pæn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>严厉批评</td><td>pan a new film<br>严厉批评一部新片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics panned the film for its clumsy dialogue and predictable ending.  影评人严厉批评这部电影对话笨拙、结局可预料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blame</td><td>/ˈbleɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>责备，归咎于</td><td>blame someone for something<br>因某事责怪某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't blame me for the broken window.  别把窗户破了怪在我头上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 censure</td><td>/ˈsenʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>公开表示反对，谴责</td><td>a vote of censure<br>谴责性投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council voted to censure the mayor for his conduct.  议会投票谴责市长的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condemn</td><td>/kənˈdem/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责（…为不道德的、邪恶的）</td><td>condemn the attack<br>谴责这次袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 World leaders quickly condemned the attack on the hospital.  各国领导人迅速谴责了对医院的袭击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local leaders publicly denounced the corruption exposed by the newspaper.  当地领导人公开谴责报纸曝光的腐败行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dispraise</del></td><td>/dɪˈspreɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>贬低，责难</td><td>dispraise a rival's work<br>贬低对手的作品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers dispraised the novel for its clumsy final chapter.  评论者因结尾笨拙而贬低这部小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reprehend</del></td><td>/reprɪˈhend/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>reprehend such conduct<br>谴责这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee did not hesitate to reprehend the officer for his careless report.  委员会毫不犹豫地谴责了那名官员草率的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rave</td><td>/reɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>狂热赞扬；（发疯般地）怒吼</td><td>rave about the film<br>对影片赞不绝口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics raved about the film for weeks after its quiet premiere.  影片低调首映后，评论家连续数周对它赞不绝口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>panache</b></td><td>/pəˈnæʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>神气，派头；张扬的自信</td><td>perform with panache<br>表演得张扬出彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered the closing argument with remarkable panache and confidence.  她以非凡的张扬气派和自信做了结案陈词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humility</td><td>/hjuˈmɪlɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，谦卑</td><td>accept with humility<br>谦逊地接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He accepted the prize with genuine humility and short thanks.  他以真诚的谦逊和简短的致谢接受了奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unremarkable behavior</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈmɑrkəbəl bɪˈheɪvjər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平淡无奇的举止</td><td>years of unremarkable behavior<br>多年平淡无奇的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unremarkable behavior at school gave no hint of later fame.  他在校时平淡的表现丝毫看不出日后的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dullness</td><td>/ˈdʌlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>乏味，单调；迟钝</td><td>the dullness of the lecture<br>讲座的乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dullness of the report hid several important findings.  这份报告的乏味掩盖了几项重要发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pandemic</b></td><td>/pænˈdemɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>大范围流行的；大流行病</td><td>a pandemic of rumors<br>谣言的大范围流传</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A pandemic of wild rumors swept the campus within a single afternoon.  一个下午之内，各种狂想谣言就在校园里大范围流传。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 limited</td><td>/ˈlɪmɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有限的，受限制的</td><td>limited resources<br>有限的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With limited time we could visit only two of the temples.  时间有限，我们只能参观其中两座寺庙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pandemonium</b></td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluster</td><td>/ˈblʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>狂妄自大地大声说；喧闹的状态</td><td>bluster and threaten<br>大声吓唬威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager began to bluster and threaten when questioned closely.  被仔细追问时，经理开始狂妄自大地大声吓唬威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bustle</td><td>/ˈbʌsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>忙乱，喧闹；（快速地）行走，奔忙</td><td>the bustle of the street<br>街上的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I love the bustle of the market on Saturday mornings.  我喜欢周六早晨市场上的喧闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fuss</td><td>/fʌs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小题大做，抱怨；忙乱</td><td>make a fuss about<br>为…大惊小怪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't make a fuss about a tiny stain on the tablecloth.  别为桌布上一小块污渍小题大做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hubbub</td><td>/ˈhʌbəb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，嘈杂；骚动</td><td>the hubbub of the market<br>市场的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We could barely talk above the hubbub of the crowd.  在人群的喧闹中我们几乎无法交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 moil</del></td><td>/mɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>骚动，混乱；苦干</td><td>the moil of the city<br>城市的喧扰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He escaped the moil of the city for a quiet island.  他逃离城市的喧扰，去了个安静的小岛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pother</del></td><td>/ˈpɑðər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，喧嚷；纷扰</td><td>raise a pother<br>引起一阵骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement raised a pother among the shareholders.  这则公告在股东中引起一阵骚动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruckus</td><td>/ˈrʌkəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，骚动</td><td>cause a ruckus<br>引起骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision caused a ruckus at the neighborhood meeting.  这一决定在社区会议上引起骚动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ruction</del></td><td>/ˈrʌkʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吵闹，骚乱</td><td>start a ruction<br>闹出一场风波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark started a ruction in the newsroom.  他不慎的一句话在编辑部闹出一场风波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serene</td><td>/səˈriːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，安详的</td><td>a serene smile<br>安详的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She watched the serene surface of the lake at dawn without speaking.  她默默看着黎明时宁静的湖面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>panegyric</b></td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accolade</td><td>/ˈækəleɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>赞扬，荣誉；奖励</td><td>win the highest accolade<br>赢得最高荣誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel received accolades from critics on both continents.  这部小说获得了两大洲评论家的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 citation</td><td>/saɪˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>表彰，嘉奖；引文</td><td>a citation for bravery<br>英勇嘉奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She received a citation for bravery after the rescue.  救援之后她获得了英勇嘉奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commendation</td><td>/ˌkɑːmənˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>表扬，嘉奖</td><td>receive a commendation for bravery<br>因勇敢获得嘉奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer received a commendation for rescuing the trapped driver.  这名警官因救出被困司机而获嘉奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dithyramb</del></td><td>/ˈdɪθəræm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热情洋溢的赞歌</td><td>a dithyramb to spring<br>献给春天的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His review was less an essay than a dithyramb to the singer.  他的评论不像文章，更像献给歌手的狂热赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eulogium</del></td><td>/juˈloʊdʒiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>颂词，赞美之辞</td><td>deliver a warm eulogium<br>致热情的颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old professor delivered a graceful eulogium for his student.  老教授为学生致了一篇优雅的颂词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hymn</td><td>/hɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞歌，赞美诗</td><td>sing a hymn<br>唱赞美诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole congregation stood up to sing a hymn of thanksgiving.  全体教众站起身来唱一首感恩的赞美诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paean</td><td>/ˈpiːən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐颂，赞扬</td><td>a paean to freedom<br>对自由的赞歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review read like a paean to the young violinist and her talent.  那篇评论读来像是对这位年轻小提琴手及其才华的赞歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 anathema</td><td>/əˈnæθəmə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>令人讨厌的人或事</td><td>be anathema to someone<br>是某人极厌恶之物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Compromise of any kind was anathema to the older party leaders.  任何形式的妥协都是老一辈党内领袖极为厌恶之事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 denunciation</td><td>/dɪˌnʌnsiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，公开指责</td><td>a public denunciation<br>公开的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was a fierce denunciation of the new policy.  她的演讲是对新政策的猛烈谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 condemnation</td><td>/ˌkɑːndemˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>widespread condemnation<br>广泛的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The raid drew swift condemnation from neighboring states.  这次袭击迅速招致邻国的谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>panoramic</b></td><td>/ˌpænəˈræmɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>全景的</td><td>a panoramic bay view<br>海湾的全景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the rooftop we enjoyed a panoramic view of the harbor at sunset.  从屋顶上我们欣赏到日落时港口的全景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compendious</td><td>/kəmˈpɛndiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明扼要的</td><td>a compendious reference guide<br>简明扼要的参考手册</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote a compendious history of the city in ninety pages.  他用九十页写成了一部简明的城市史。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complete</td><td>/kəmˈpliːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完全的，彻底的；完成</td><td>a complete failure<br>彻底的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first attempt was a complete failure.  第一次尝试彻底失败了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comprehensive</td><td>/ˌkɑːmprɪˈhensɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全面的，综合的</td><td>a comprehensive survey<br>全面的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee produced a comprehensive report on water quality.  委员会就水质提出了一份全面的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cyclopedic</del></td><td>/ˌsaɪkləˈpidɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>包罗万象的，博大的</td><td>cyclopedic knowledge of birds<br>对鸟类的百科式学识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had cyclopedic knowledge of every bird on the coast.  他对海岸上每种鸟都有百科式的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 embracive</del></td><td>/ɛmˈbreɪsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>包容广泛的，涵盖面广的</td><td>an embracive definition<br>涵盖面很广的定义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The law uses an embracive definition of public property.  该法律对公共财产采用涵盖面很广的定义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhaustive</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːstɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详尽的，彻底的</td><td>an exhaustive search<br>彻底的搜查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After an exhaustive review, the board found no wrongdoing.  经过详尽审查，董事会未发现不当行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thorough</td><td>/ˈθɜːroʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彻底的，详尽的</td><td>a thorough investigation<br>彻底的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors carried out a thorough search of every storage room.  检查员对每间储藏室都做了彻底搜查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 in-depth</td><td>/ˌɪnˈdepθ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深入的，详尽的</td><td>an in-depth interview<br>深度访谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine ran an in-depth study of village schools.  这家杂志刊登了对乡村学校的深入研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 narrow</td><td>/ˈnæroʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>狭窄的；狭隘的</td><td>a narrow outlook<br>狭隘的眼界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His narrow reading habits left him ignorant of modern poetry.  他狭窄的阅读习惯使他对现代诗一无所知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>provenance</b></td><td>/ˈprɑːvənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>来源，起源</td><td>the provenance of the vase<br>花瓶的来源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts still dispute the provenance of the vase found in the tomb.  专家们对墓中出土花瓶的来源仍有争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derivation</td><td>/ˌderəˈveɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>起源，由来；词源</td><td>the derivation of the word<br>这个词的词源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars dispute the derivation of the village's odd name.  学者们对这个村子怪名的由来有争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fountain</td><td>/ˈfaʊntən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>源泉，来源；喷泉</td><td>a fountain of knowledge<br>知识的源泉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old librarian was a fountain of local history.  那位老图书馆员是地方史的活源泉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inception</td><td>/ɪnˈsepʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>开端，开始</td><td>since its inception<br>自创立以来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club has doubled in size since its inception three years ago.  这个俱乐部自三年前创立以来规模翻了一倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 origin</td><td>/ˈɔːrədʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>起源，由来，出身</td><td>the origin of the custom<br>这一习俗的起源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars still argue about the origin of the strange name.  学者们仍在争论这个奇怪名字的来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 root</td><td>/ˈruːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使扎根；根，根源</td><td>deeply rooted in tradition<br>深深扎根于传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The custom is rooted in the farming life of the valley.  这一习俗根植于山谷的农耕生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 source</td><td>/ˈsɔːrs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>来源，源头，出处</td><td>the source of the problem<br>问题的根源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers traced the leak to its source under the road.  工程师把漏水追查到路面下的源头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wellspring</td><td>/ˈwelˌsprɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>源泉，源头</td><td>a wellspring of inspiration<br>灵感的源泉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Childhood summers became the wellspring of all his novels.  童年的夏天成了他所有小说的源泉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>paradigm</b></td><td>/ˈpærədaɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>典范，模范</td><td>a paradigm of good design<br>优秀设计的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This small library is a paradigm of thoughtful modern design.  这座小图书馆是精心现代设计的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 archetype</td><td>/ˈɑːrkɪtaɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型，典型</td><td>the archetype of a hero<br>英雄的典型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sherlock Holmes remains the archetype of the brilliant private detective.  夏洛克·福尔摩斯仍是天才私家侦探的原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 example</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæmpəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>范例，典范；例子</td><td>set a good example<br>作出好榜样</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chapel is a fine example of early Gothic building.  这座礼拜堂是早期哥特建筑的绝佳范例。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exemplar</td><td>/ɪɡˈzemplɑr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>典范，模范</td><td>an exemplar of restraint<br>克制的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her career remains an exemplar of patient scientific work.  她的生涯至今仍是耐心科研的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ideal</td><td>/aɪˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>理想，典范；理想的</td><td>an ideal solution<br>理想的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remains the ideal of quiet courage for many students.  她仍是许多学生心中沉静勇气的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 model</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>典范，模范；模型</td><td>a model of efficiency<br>效率的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic became a model of efficiency for the region.  这家诊所成了全区效率的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pattern</td><td>/ˈpætərn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>范例，样板；模式</td><td>a pattern to follow<br>可效法的样板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform became a pattern for other cities to follow.  这项改革成了其他城市效法的样板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paragon</td><td>/ˈpærəɡɑːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>优秀模范；把…比作；显示相似</td><td>a paragon of patience<br>耐心的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old librarian was a paragon of patience with every noisy student.  那位老图书馆员对每个吵闹的学生都是耐心的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>paradise</b></td><td>/ˈpærədaɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>乐园，天堂；极大的快乐</td><td>a paradise of quiet reading<br>静静读书的乐境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reading quietly by the lake all afternoon was pure paradise for her.  整个下午在湖边静静读书对她来说是极大的快乐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elation</td><td>/ɪˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>得意，兴高采烈</td><td>a feeling of elation<br>兴高采烈之感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her elation faded once she read the fine print carefully.  她仔细读了小字条款后，兴奋就消退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 euphoria</td><td>/juːˈfɔːriə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>极度愉快，狂喜</td><td>a mood of national euphoria<br>全国上下的狂喜情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The euphoria after the victory faded within a couple of days.  胜利后的极度愉快在几天之内就消退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhilaration</td><td>/ɪɡˌzɪləˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>兴奋，极度愉快</td><td>a sense of exhilaration<br>一种兴奋感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt pure exhilaration as the glider rose above the valley.  滑翔机升到山谷上方时，她感到无比兴奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intoxication</td><td>/ˌɪntɑːksəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极度兴奋，陶醉；醉酒</td><td>the intoxication of success<br>成功带来的陶醉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intoxication of victory faded once the hard rebuilding work began.  艰难重建开始后，胜利的陶醉便消退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapture</td><td>/ˈræptʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>极度欢喜，狂喜</td><td>listen in rapture<br>如痴如醉地聆听</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience listened in rapture as the old singer began her final song.  老歌手唱起最后一曲时，观众听得如痴如醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rhapsody</td><td>/ˈræpsədi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂喜，热情赞美；狂想曲</td><td>go into rhapsodies<br>大加赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He went into rhapsodies about the noodles he ate in Osaka.  他对在大阪吃的面条大加赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transport</td><td>/trænsˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>运输，运送</td><td>transport the goods<br>运送货物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trucks transport fresh produce to the city every morning.  卡车每天早晨把新鲜农产品运往城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dejection</td><td>/dɪˈdʒɛkʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>沮丧，忧郁</td><td>a look of dejection<br>沮丧的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat in silent dejection after the results came out.  成绩出来后他沮丧地默默坐着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 depression</td><td>/dɪˈpreʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>沮丧，抑郁；萧条</td><td>sink into deep depression<br>陷入深度抑郁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After losing his job he slid into a long depression.  失业之后，他陷入了长久的抑郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloominess</td><td>/ˈɡlumɪnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>阴郁，忧愁</td><td>the gloominess of the news<br>消息的阴郁</td></tr>
